=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a tree rooted at node 0, consisting of n nodes numbered from 0 to n - 1. The tree is represented by an array parent of size n, where parent[i] is the parent of node i. Since node 0 is the root, parent[0] == -1.
You are also given a string s of length n, where s[i] is the character assigned to node i.
Consider an empty string dfsStr, and define a recursive function dfs(int x) that takes a node x as a parameter and performs the following steps in order:

Iterate over each child y of x in increasing order of their numbers, and call dfs(y).
Add the character s[x] to the end of the string dfsStr.

Note that dfsStr is shared across all recursive calls of dfs.
You need to find a boolean array answer of size n, where for each index i from 0 to n - 1, you do the following:

Empty the string dfsStr and call dfs(i).
If the resulting string dfsStr is a palindrome, then set answer[i] to true. Otherwise, set answer[i] to false.

Return the array answer.
 
Example 1:


Input: parent = [-1,0,0,1,1,2], s = "aababa"
Output: [true,true,false,true,true,true]
Explanation:

Calling dfs(0) results in the string dfsStr = "abaaba", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(1) results in the string dfsStr = "aba", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(2) results in the string dfsStr = "ab", which is not a palindrome.
Calling dfs(3) results in the string dfsStr = "a", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(4) results in the string dfsStr = "b", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(5) results in the string dfsStr = "a", which is a palindrome.


Example 2:


Input: parent = [-1,0,0,0,0], s = "aabcb"
Output: [true,true,true,true,true]
Explanation:
Every call on dfs(x) results in a palindrome string.

 
Constraints:

n == parent.length == s.length
1 <= n <= 10^5
0 <= parent[i] <= n - 1 for all i >= 1.
parent[0] == -1
parent represents a valid tree.
s consists only of lowercase English letters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        
```

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Then Manacher on P.

Manacher algorithm for palindrome substrings:
Given string P length n. Compute d1[i] = number of odd palindromes centered at i, i.e., radius count including center: palindrome radius in chars; d1[i] >=1. For substring [l,r) length len = r-l. It is palindrome iff:
- if len odd: let center = (l+r)//2, radius = len//2 +1. Need d1[center] >= radius.
- if len even: center between i-1 and i? d2[i] = number of even palindromes centered between i-1 and i, radius in pairs? Standard d2[i] = radius length in characters? Let's recall.

Manacher:
d1[i]: odd radius, number of palindromes with center i, radius k such that s[i-k+1..i+k-1] palindrome? Actually d1[i] = max k such that s[i-k+1..i+k-1] palindrome length 2k-1. So d1[i] = count of odd palindrome radii including center. For substring [l,r) odd length len=2k-1, center c=(l+r)//2, need d1[c] >= k = (len+1)//2.
d2[i]: even radius, number of palindromes centered between i-1 and i, radius k such that s[i-k..i+k-1] palindrome length 2k. d2[i] = max k. For substring [l,r) even length len=2k, center between c-1 and c where c = (l+r)//2? Actually for even substring [l,r), center between c-1 and c where c = (l+r)//2? Let's derive. If l=0,r=2, center between 0 and1, c=1. d2[1] max k=1. If l=1,r=3, center between 1 and2, c=2. So c = (l+r)//2? For len=2, (1+3)//2=2, yes. For len=4 [1,5], center between2 and3, c=3? (1+5)//2=3. So c = (l+r)//2. Need d2[c] >= len//2. Standard d2[i] centered between i-1 and i. So c = (l+r)//2. Good.

Alternatively use transformed string with separators to unify, but d1/d2 fine.

Need compute d1,d2 for P. We can implement standard O(n):
def manacher(s):
    n=len(s)
    d1=[0]*n
    l=0; r=-1
    for i in range(n):
        k=1 if i>r else min(d1[l+r-i], r-i+1)
        while i-k>=0 and i+k<n and s[i-k]==s[i+k]:
            k+=1
        d1[i]=k
        if i+k-1 > r:
            l=i-k+1; r=i+k-1
    d2=[0]*n
    l=0; r=-1
    for i in range(n):
        k=0 if i>r else min(d2[l+r-i+1], r-i+1)
        while i-k-1>=0 and i+k<n and s[i-k-1]==s[i+k]:
            k+=1
        d2[i]=k
        if i+k-1 > r:
            l=i-k; r=i+k-1
    return d1,d2
Need check d2 indexing mirror: standard from cp-algorithms:
vector<int> d2(n);
for (int i=0,l=0,r=-1; i<n; i++) {
    int k = (i > r) ? 0 : min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1);
    while (i + k < n && i - k - 1 >= 0 && s[i + k] == s[i - k - 1]) {
        k++;
    }
    d2[i] = k;
    if (i + k - 1 > r) {
        l = i - k;
        r = i + k - 1;
    }
}
Yes.

        c=(l+r)//2
        ans.append(d2[c] >= length//2)

Need compute intervals. Let's design iterative postorder and intervals in one pass.

Pseudo:
n = len(parent)
children=[[] for _ in range(n)]
for i in range(1,n):
    children[parent[i]].append(i)
# children sorted because i increasing.

sz=[0]*n
intervals=[None]*n
P_chars=[]
stack=[(0, False)]
while stack:
    x, exited = stack.pop()
    if not exited:
        stack.append((x, True))
        # push children in reverse order to process increasing
        for y in reversed(children[x]):
            stack.append((y, False))
    else:
        total=1
        for y in children[x]:
            total += sz[y]
        sz[x]=total
        # before append, P length L
        # append char
        P_chars.append(s[x])
        r = len(P_chars)  # after append, 1-indexed count
        l = r - sz[x]
        intervals[x]=(l,r)
P = ''.join(P_chars)

Need verify interval l nonnegative. Since at exit x, all descendants appended, so P length before append >= sz[x]-1. After append length L_before+1, r-l = sz[x]. l = L_before+1 - sz[x] >=0. Good.

Now, let's think about exact palindrome check with Manacher. Need implement correctly.

Manacher details:

def manacher(s):
    n = len(s)
    d1 = [0]*n
    l=0; r=-1
    for i in range(n):
        if i > r:
            k=1
        else:
            mirror = l + r - i
            k = min(d1[mirror], r - i + 1)
        while i-k >=0 and i+k < n and s[i-k] == s[i+k]:
            k += 1
        d1[i] = k
        if i + k - 1 > r:
            l = i - k + 1
            r = i + k - 1
    d2 = [0]*n
    l=0; r=-1
    for i in range(n):
        if i > r:
            k=0
        else:
            mirror = l + r - i + 1
            k = min(d2[mirror], r - i + 1)
        while i-k-1 >=0 and i+k < n and s[i-k-1] == s[i+k]:
            k += 1
        d2[i] = k
        if i + k - 1 > r:
            l = i - k
            r = i + k - 1
    return d1,d2

    d2[i] = k;
    if (i + k - 1 > r) {
        l = i - k;
        r = i + k - 1;
    }

Let's think if there is any possibility that T_i is not contiguous in P if children sorted increasing? In DFS, yes. Let's formalize: The recursive calls in dfs(0) for a node x: When dfs(0) reaches x, it calls dfs(y) for each child y in order. Each dfs(y) appends exactly T_y. Then appends s[x]. No other appends occur between these calls except inside those dfs(y). Thus the appended string during the call dfs(x) is exactly T_x, and it is appended contiguously to global dfsStr. For a string S length n. d1[i] = maximum k such that S[i-k+1 ... i+k-1] is palindrome length 2k-1. For substring [l,r), length len = r-l. If len odd, center c = (l+r)//2. Example l=0,r=5,len5,c=2. Palindrome length 5 => k=3. Need d1[c] >= 3. (len+1)//2=3. Good. If len=1, c=l, k=1, d1>=1. Good.

d2[i] = maximum k such that S[i-k ... i+k-1] is palindrome length 2k. For substring [l,r), len even, center between c-1 and c where c = (l+r)//2. Example l=0,r=4,len4,c=2. Palindrome S[0..3] centered between1 and2? Wait center between c-1=1 and c=2. Standard d2[2] centered between1 and2? Let's check standard definition: d2[i] is radius of even palindrome centered between i-1 and i. Yes for i=2 center between1 and2. The palindrome S[0..3] length4 has center between1 and2, so d2[2]>=2. Good. For l=1,r=5,len4,c=3, center between2 and3, d2[3]>=2. For l=0,r=2,len2,c=1, center between0 and1, d2[1]>=1. Good. So c=(l+r)//2. Need d2[c] >= len//2. Good.

Let's write code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        n = len(parent)
        children = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for i in range(1, n):
            children[parent[i]].append(i)

        sz = [0] * n
        left = [0] * n
        right = [0] * n
        post_chars = []

        stack = [(0, False)]
        while stack:
            x, exited = stack.pop()
            if not exited:
                stack.append((x, True))
                # push children in reverse so they are processed in increasing order
                for y in reversed(children[x]):
                    stack.append((y, False))
            else:
                total = 1
                for y in children[x]:
                    total += sz[y]
                sz[x] = total
                post_chars.append(s[x])
                r = len(post_chars)
                l = r - sz[x]
                left[x] = l
                right[x] = r

        p = ''.join(post_chars)

        # Manacher
        d1 = [0] * n
        l = 0
        r = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 1
            else:
                k = min(d1[l + r - i], r - i + 1)
            while i - k >= 0 and i + k < n and p[i - k] == p[i + k]:
                k += 1
            d1[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k + 1
                r = i + k - 1

        d2 = [0] * n
        l = 0
        r = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 0
            else:
                k = min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1)
            while i - k - 1 >= 0 and i + k < n and p[i - k - 1] == p[i + k]:
                k += 1
            d2[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k
                r = i + k - 1

        ans = [False] * n
        for i in range(n):
            length = right[i] - left[i]
            if length & 1:
                c = (left[i] + right[i]) // 2
                ans[i] = d1[c] >= (length + 1) // 2
            else:
                c = (left[i] + right[i]) // 2
                ans[i] = d2[c] >= length // 2
        return ans

Need verify Manacher d2 mirror index `l + r - i + 1` can be out of bounds? Standard. But let's test with small examples manually.

i=0 k0 no update.
i=1 k0 while p0==p1? a,b false d2[1]=0, i+k-1=0 > -1 -> l=1,r=0.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        n = len(parent)
        children = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for i in range(1, n):
            children[parent[i]].append(i)

        size = [0] * n
        left = [0] * n
        right = [0] * n
        post = []

        stack = [(0, 0)]  # 0 enter, 1 exit
        while stack:
            x, state = stack.pop()
            if state == 0:
                stack.append((x, 1))
                for y in reversed(children[x]):
                    stack.append((y, 0))
            else:
                total = 1
                for y in children[x]:
                    total += size[y]
                size[x] = total
                post.append(s[x])
                r = len(post)
                l = r - size[x]
                left[x] = l
                right[x] = r

        p = ''.join(post)

        # odd manacher
        d1 = [0] * n
        l = 0
        r = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 1
            else:
                k = min(d1[l + r - i], r - i + 1)
            while i - k >= 0 and i + k < n and p[i - k] == p[i + k]:
                k += 1
            d1[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k + 1
                r = i + k - 1

        # even manacher
        d2 = [0] * n
        l = 0
        r = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 0
            else:
                k = min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1)
            while i - k - 1 >= 0 and i + k < n and p[i - k - 1] == p[i + k]:
                k += 1
            d2[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k
                r = i + k - 1

        ans = [False] * n
        for i in range(n):
            length = right[i] - left[i]
            mid = (left[i] + right[i]) // 2
            if length & 1:
                ans[i] = d1[mid] >= (length + 1) // 2
            else:
                ans[i] = d2[mid] >= length // 2
        return ans

Need check variable `l` and `r` in Manacher: We use `l` and `r` after previously using `l`? In code, after building P, we set `l = 0; r = -1`. But `left` and `right` arrays are separate. Good.

Now, let's think about if `stack = [(0, 0)]` and state 0/1; if n=0? n>=1. Good.

Good. So order is 1,2,3. Good.

i=0: k0, d2[0]=0, i+k-1=-1 > -1 false.
i=1: i>r(-1), k0, while p0==p1? a,b false, d2[1]=0, i+k-1=0 > -1 true, l=1,r=0.
i=2: i>r? 2>0 true, k0, while p1==p2? b,c false, d2[2]=0, i+k-1=1 > 0 true, l=2,r=1.
d2=[0,0,0]. Good. No out-of-bounds because when i>r, no mirror. For i=2, r=0, i>r. Good.

i=0 k1 d1[0]=1, update l=0,r=0.
i=1 i>r (1>0) k1, while p0==p2? a,c false, d1[1]=1, update i+k-1=1 >0 l=1,r=1.
i=2 i>r (2>1) k1, while i-k=1, i+k=3 out, d1[2]=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with d1 mirror when r-i+1 maybe 0? If i<=r, r-i+1 >=1. Good.

Let's write final code with start and size:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        n = len(parent)
        children = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for i in range(1, n):
            children[parent[i]].append(i)

        size = [0] * n
        start = [0] * n
        post = []

        stack = [(0, 0)]
        while stack:
            x, state = stack.pop()
            if state == 0:
                stack.append((x, 1))
                for y in reversed(children[x]):
                    stack.append((y, 0))
            else:
                total = 1
                for y in children[x]:
                    total += size[y]
                size[x] = total
                post.append(s[x])
                start[x] = len(post) - total

        p = ''.join(post)
        # Manacher...
        ans = [False] * n
        for i in range(n):
            length = size[i]
            mid = start[i] + length // 2
            if length & 1:
                ans[i] = d1[mid] >= (length + 1) // 2
            else:
                ans[i] = d2[mid] >= length // 2
        return ans

Need check `start[x] = len(post) - total` after append. For node with total=size[x]. Good.

node0 append len6 total6 start0.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
with code block.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        n = len(parent)

        children = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for i in range(1, n):
            children[parent[i]].append(i)

        size = [0] * n
        start = [0] * n
        post = []

        # Iterative postorder DFS: state 0 = enter, state 1 = exit
        stack = [(0, 0)]
        while stack:
            x, state = stack.pop()
            if state == 0:
                stack.append((x, 1))
                # Push children in reverse so they are processed in increasing order
                for y in reversed(children[x]):
                    stack.append((y, 0))
            else:
                total = 1
                for y in children[x]:
                    total += size[y]
                size[x] = total

                post.append(s[x])
                start[x] = len(post) - total

        p = ''.join(post)

        # Manacher: odd-length palindromes
        d1 = [0] * n
        l, r = 0, -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 1
            else:
                k = min(d1[l + r - i], r - i + 1)

            while i - k >= 0 and i + k < n and p[i - k] == p[i + k]:
                k += 1

            d1[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k + 1
                r = i + k - 1

        # Manacher: even-length palindromes
        d2 = [0] * n
        l, r = 0, -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 0
            else:
                k = min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1)

            while i - k - 1 >= 0 and i + k < n and p[i - k - 1] == p[i + k]:
                k += 1

            d2[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k
                r = i + k - 1

        ans = [False] * n
        for i in range(n):
            length = size[i]
            mid = start[i] + length // 2

            if length & 1:
                ans[i] = d1[mid] >= (length + 1) // 2
            else:
                ans[i] = d2[mid] >= length // 2

        return ans
```
